Тема: № 80 Отчётность и презентация результатов работы (Reporting and Presenting Work Results)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. metrics [ˈmetrɪks] — метрики, показатели
2. to highlight [ˈhaɪlaɪt] — выделять, подчеркивать
3. key takeaway [kiː ˈteɪkweɪ] — главный вывод
4. data visualization [ˈdeɪtə ˌvɪʒuəlaɪˈzeɪʃn] — визуализация данных
5. concise [kənˈsaɪs] — краткий, лаконичный
6. to deliver [dɪˈlɪvə] — представлять, доносить (информацию)

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~440 слов.
"Alex, the quarterly review is tomorrow," said Elena, the marketing director. "Do you have the performance report ready?"

"Yes, Elena," Alex replied, handing her a thick stack of papers. "I included every single metric, every email open rate, and all the website traffic data from the last three months."

Elena flipped through the pages and sighed. "Alex, this is 40 pages long. The executive team doesn't have time to read a novel. They need a concise summary."

"But I worked so hard on gathering all this data!" Alex protested. "I thought more details meant a better report."

"Data is useless if you don't tell a story with it," Elena explained gently. "Your job is not to dump information. Your job is to deliver insights. Let’s look at this together. What is the key takeaway from all these numbers?"

Alex thought for a moment. "Well, our social media campaign increased brand awareness by 30%, but it didn't lead to many actual sales."

"Exactly!" Elena said, her eyes lighting up. "That is your key takeaway. Now, highlight that on the first page. Use data visualization, like a simple bar chart, to show the 30% growth. Then, in the next section, explain why the sales didn't follow and propose a solution."

"So, I should cut out the minor details?" Alex asked.

"Yes," Elena nodded. "Keep the detailed data in an appendix for those who want to dig deeper. But your main presentation should be short, visual, and focused on results and future actions."

Alex went back to his desk and completely reworked the report. He removed the walls of text. He created three clear charts. He wrote a one-page executive summary that highlighted the main successes and the one major problem they needed to solve.

The next day, during the meeting, Alex presented his new report. The executives were engaged. They asked thoughtful questions about his proposed solution, and the CEO nodded in approval.

"Great job, Alex," the CEO said at the end. "Clear, concise, and actionable."

After the meeting, Elena smiled at him. "See? You didn't just give them numbers. You gave them a clear picture of where we stand and where we need to go."

Alex realized that good reporting isn't about proving how much work you did. It’s about respecting your audience's time and delivering the insights that actually matter for the business.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why was Elena not satisfied with Alex’s first draft of the report?
2. What was the key takeaway Alex discovered in the data?
3. How did the executives react to Alex’s final presentation?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Alex gave Elena a 40-page report with every single... (metric).
Elena said it was too long and asked for a... (concise) summary.
She explained that he needed to tell a story and deliver... (insights), not just data.
Alex realized the... (key takeaway) was that brand awareness grew, but sales did not.
He decided to... (highlight) this point and use... (data visualization) like charts.
He moved the minor details to an appendix and kept the main presentation short.
The executives loved the report because it was clear and... (actionable).
Alex learned that good reporting is about respecting the audience's... (time).

Тема: № 81 Кросс-функциональное взаимодействие (Cross-Functional Collaboration)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. cross-functional [ˌkrɒs ˈfʌŋkʃənl] — кросс-функциональный, межведомственный
2. silo [ˈsaɪləʊ] — изолированная группа (работать в «вакууме»)
3. alignment [əˈlaɪnmənt] — согласованность, единство целей
4. to bridge the gap [brɪdʒ ðə ɡæp] — преодолеть разрыв, наладить связь
5. common goal [ˈkɒmən ɡəʊl] — общая цель
6. to compromise [ˈkɒmprəmaɪz] — идти на компромисс

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~450 слов.
The new mobile app launch was delayed again. In the conference room, the atmosphere was tense. 

"The design is too complex," argued Tom, the lead engineer. "My team can’t code this in two weeks. You developers always think design is magic!"

Lisa, the head of design, crossed her arms. "And you engineers never care about the user experience! If we simplify it, the app will look cheap. We are working in silos, and it’s ruining the project."

David, the project manager, stepped in. "Okay, everyone, stop. This blame game is not helping. We are a cross-functional team, which means we need to work together, not against each other."

"But their demands are unrealistic," Tom muttered.

"Let’s find some alignment," David said calmly. "What is our common goal here?"

"To launch a high-quality app on time," Lisa and Tom said in unison, though they still looked annoyed.

"Great," David said. "So, fighting doesn't achieve that. We need to bridge the gap between design and engineering. Tom, what specific part of the design is causing the delay?"

"The animated transitions on the home screen," Tom replied. "They require custom code that we don't have time to build and test."

David turned to Lisa. "Lisa, is that animation essential for the user experience, or is it just a nice-to-have?"

Lisa thought for a moment. "It’s nice-to-have. The core functionality is more important."

"Perfect," David smiled. "Here is the compromise. Lisa, your team will simplify the animations for the launch version. Tom, your team will commit to implementing the simplified version perfectly within the two-week deadline. After the launch, we can schedule an update for the advanced animations. Does that work?"

Tom nodded. "Yes, I can agree to that."

"Me too," Lisa said, her tone softening. "Thank you for facilitating this, David."

Over the next two weeks, the teams communicated daily. Designers sat with developers to answer questions instantly. There were no more silos. 

On launch day, the app went live without a single critical bug. The user reviews praised both the beautiful interface and the smooth performance.

During the celebration, Tom raised his glass to Lisa. "Sorry for calling your designs magic. They are actually pretty good."

Lisa laughed. "And sorry for thinking you don't care about users. You guys saved the launch."

David watched them, satisfied. He knew that cross-functional collaboration wasn't about everyone agreeing on everything. It was about respecting different expertise, communicating openly, and compromising to achieve a common goal.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why were Tom and Lisa arguing in the conference room?
2. What did David identify as their common goal?
3. What compromise did the teams reach regarding the app design?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, the app launch was delayed because the design and engineering teams were working in... (silos).
Tom complained about complex design, and Lisa complained about poor... (user experience).
David stepped in to stop the blame game and find... (alignment).
He reminded them of their... (common goal): launching a high-quality app on time.
They identified the animated transitions as the main... (problem).
Lisa agreed to simplify the animations, and Tom agreed to code the... (simplified) version quickly.
The teams communicated daily to... (bridge the gap) between their departments.
The launch was successful, and they learned that collaboration requires respect and... (compromise).

Тема: № 82 Работа в условиях неопределённости и быстрых изменений (Working in Uncertainty and Rapid Change)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. uncertainty [ʌnˈsɜːtnti] — неопределенность
2. agile [ˈædʒaɪl] — гибкий, проворный (методология)
3. to pivot [ˈpɪvət] — резко менять направление, перестраиваться
4. flexible [ˈfleksəbl] — гибкий, адаптивный
5. to adapt [əˈdæpt] — адаптироваться, приспосабливаться
6. contingency plan [kənˈtɪndʒənsi plæn] — план действий на непредвиденный случай

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~440 слов.
Anna, the founder of a small tech startup, called an emergency meeting on a Tuesday morning. Her face was pale.

"Team, I have bad news," she began. "A major competitor just launched a free version of our main product. They have more resources and a bigger marketing budget. Our projected sales for this quarter are now in serious uncertainty."

The room went silent. Mark, the lead developer, looked worried. "Are we going to shut down?"

"No," Anna said firmly. "But we cannot stick to our original plan. We need to be agile. We have to pivot our strategy immediately."

"How?" asked Sarah, the marketing manager. "We can't compete with 'free'."

"We don't compete on price; we compete on value," Anna explained. "Our free competitor has a generic product. Our strength is personalized customer service and advanced customization. We need to pivot our messaging to target premium clients who need those specific features."

"That’s a big change," Mark noted. "Can we adapt our software that quickly?"

"We have to be flexible," Anna replied. "We already have a contingency plan for rapid updates. Mark, can your team release a new 'Premium Customization' module in two weeks?"

Mark thought for a moment, doing mental calculations. "If we drop the minor bug fixes and focus only on the new module... yes, we can do it."

"Excellent," Anna said. "Sarah, I need you to rewrite the website copy and launch a new email campaign targeting our existing high-value clients by Friday. We will offer them an exclusive early access to this new module."

"It’s risky, but I like it," Sarah said, already opening her laptop. "Let’s do it."

For the next two weeks, the team worked with intense focus. There was no time for long meetings or perfectionism. They tested ideas quickly, gathered feedback, and adapted on the fly. 

On Friday, Sarah launched the campaign. By Monday morning, they had received fifty replies from existing clients upgrading to the premium tier. 

"We did it," Anna said, reading the numbers with a huge smile. "We didn't just survive the change; we used it to improve our product."

Mark high-fived Sarah. "I can't believe we pulled that off in two weeks."

Anna looked at her team with pride. She realized that uncertainty is inevitable in business. The companies that survive are not the ones with the perfect initial plan, but the ones that are agile enough to pivot, flexible enough to adapt, and brave enough to execute a new vision.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. What bad news did Anna share with her team?
2. How did the team decide to compete with the free competitor?
3. What did Mark and Sarah have to do in two weeks?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, a competitor launched a free product, creating... (uncertainty) for the startup.
Anna said they couldn't stick to the old plan and needed to be... (agile).
They decided to... (pivot) their strategy to focus on premium clients and customization.
Mark agreed to build a new module quickly because they had a... (contingency plan).
Sarah rewrote the marketing copy to target... (high-value) clients.
The team had to be... (flexible) and work without perfectionism.
The campaign was successful, and many clients... (upgraded) to the premium tier.
Anna learned that surviving change requires the ability to... (adapt) and execute a new vision.

Тема: № 83 Финансовая грамотность и инвестирование для начинающих (Financial Literacy and Investing for Beginners)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. compound interest [ˈkɒmpaʊnd ˈɪntrəst] — сложный процент
2. to diversify [daɪˈvɜːsɪfaɪ] — диверсифицировать (распределять риски)
3. index fund [ˈɪndeks fʌnd] — индексный фонд
4. risk tolerance [rɪsk ˈtɒlərəns] — толерантность к риску
5. to automate [ˈɔːtəmeɪt] — автоматизировать
6. long-term [ˌlɒŋ ˈtɜːm] — долгосрочный

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~450 слов.
Leo sat in the office of Mr. Davis, a certified financial planner. Leo was 25, had a stable job, and had finally saved $5,000. 

"I want to make this money grow," Leo said. "But the stock market seems like a casino. I’m afraid of losing everything."

Mr. Davis smiled warmly. "That’s a very common fear, Leo. But investing is not gambling if you do it correctly. The most powerful force in investing is compound interest."

"Compound interest?" Leo asked. 

"Yes," Mr. Davis explained. "It means you earn interest not only on your initial money but also on the interest that money has already earned. Over a long-term period, it grows exponentially. But you must start early."

"Okay, but what should I buy? Individual stocks like Apple or Tesla?"

"For a beginner, I highly recommend an index fund," Mr. Davis advised. "Instead of trying to pick one winning company, an index fund buys small pieces of hundreds of companies at once. It allows you to diversify your portfolio instantly. If one company fails, the others balance it out."

"That sounds much safer," Leo noted. "But what about my risk tolerance? I don't like seeing my account balance drop."

"Index funds are generally less volatile than individual stocks," Mr. Davis said. "However, all investments have some risk. The key is to hold them for years, not days. The market goes up and down, but historically, it always goes up over the long term."

"How do I make sure I don’t spend the money instead?" Leo asked.

"You automate it," Mr. Davis said, leaning forward. "Set up an automatic transfer from your checking account to your investment account every month. Even if it’s just $100. You won't miss the money, and you build a habit."

Leo took out his notebook and wrote down the steps: 1. Open a brokerage account. 2. Choose a broad market index fund. 3. Set up automatic monthly contributions. 

"This makes it sound so simple," Leo said, feeling a wave of relief. 

"It is simple, but it requires discipline," Mr. Davis replied. "Don't check the app every day. Let compound interest do the heavy lifting."

Six months later, Leo checked his account. The market had dipped slightly, but he didn't panic. He remembered Mr. Davis’s advice about the long term. He kept his automated transfers running. 

Leo realized that financial literacy wasn't about knowing complex Wall Street jargon. It was about understanding basic principles like diversification, compound interest, and automation, and having the patience to let time work in your favor.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why was Leo afraid of investing his $5,000?
2. What is compound interest according to Mr. Davis?
3. Why did Mr. Davis recommend an index fund for a beginner?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Leo wanted to invest but was afraid of losing his... (money).
Mr. Davis explained the power of... (compound interest) over time.
Instead of picking individual stocks, he advised Leo to buy an... (index fund).
This allows a beginner to... (diversify) their portfolio and reduce risk.
Mr. Davis asked about Leo's... (risk tolerance) and advised him to think long-term.
To build a habit, Mr. Davis suggested Leo... (automate) his monthly contributions.
Leo wrote down the steps and felt much more... (relieved / confident).
He learned that financial literacy is about basic principles and... (patience).

Тема: № 84 Здоровый сон и восстановление (Healthy Sleep and Recovery)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. sleep hygiene [sliːp ˈhaɪdʒiːn] — гигиена сна
2. screen time [skriːn taɪm] — время, проведенное у экрана
3. to unwind [ʌnˈwaɪnd] — расслабляться, отдыхать
4. circadian rhythm [sɜːˈkeɪdiən rɪðəm] — циркадный ритм
5. to recharge [ˌriːˈtʃɑːdʒ] — перезаряжаться, восстанавливать силы
6. burnout [ˈbɜːnaʊt] — эмоциональное выгорание

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~440 слов.
Emma dragged herself into the doctor’s office, yawning for the third time in five minutes. Dark circles were visible under her eyes.

"Rough week?" asked Dr. Smith, looking at her chart.

"Rough month," Emma corrected. "I’m constantly tired. I drink four cups of coffee a day, but I still feel like I’m heading for burnout. I sleep about five hours a night, but I wake up exhausted."

Dr. Smith nodded sympathetically. "Emma, you can’t cheat biology. Your body and brain need time to recharge. Let’s talk about your sleep hygiene."

"My what?" Emma asked.

"Sleep hygiene," Dr. Smith repeated. "These are the habits and environment that promote good sleep. First question: what do you do in the hour before bed?"

Emma hesitated. "I usually scroll through social media on my phone. Or I check my work emails one last time."

"There’s your problem," Dr. Smith said gently. "The blue light from your screen time suppresses melatonin, the hormone that makes you sleepy. It also tricks your circadian rhythm into thinking it’s still daytime."

"So, no phone in bed?" Emma asked.

"Exactly," Dr. Smith said. "I want you to create a wind-down routine. One hour before bed, turn off all screens. Read a physical book, take a warm shower, or do some light stretching. You need to signal to your brain that it’s time to unwind."

"What about my alarm?" Emma asked. "I use my phone for that."

"Buy a cheap, traditional alarm clock," Dr. Smith advised. "Keep your phone in another room. This prevents the temptation to check notifications if you wake up in the middle of the night."

Emma sighed. "It sounds hard. My phone is my lifeline."

"It will be difficult for the first three days," Dr. Smith admitted. "But I promise, your sleep quality will improve dramatically. And you must aim for seven to eight hours. Five is simply not enough to recover from a demanding job."

That night, Emma bought a basic alarm clock. She left her phone in the kitchen. At 10 PM, she turned off the TV and read a novel. 

The first night, she tossed and turned, missing her phone. But by the third night, she fell asleep within twenty minutes. 

A week later, Emma returned to the clinic for a follow-up. She looked brighter and more energetic. 

"Doctor, it worked," she smiled. "I’m sleeping seven hours, and I don't need coffee after 2 PM anymore. I finally feel like I can recharge."

Dr. Smith smiled back. "Sleep isn't a luxury, Emma. It’s the foundation of your health and productivity. Protect it."

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why did Emma feel like she was heading for burnout?
2. What bad habit was ruining Emma’s sleep hygiene?
3. What solution did Dr. Smith suggest for the phone problem?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Emma visited the doctor because she was constantly tired and only slept... (five) hours.
Dr. Smith explained that she needed to improve her... (sleep hygiene).
He pointed out that high... (screen time) before bed disrupts the... (circadian rhythm).
He advised Emma to create a routine to... (unwind) without electronic devices.
Emma bought a traditional alarm clock and left her phone in another... (room).
At first, it was hard, but soon she started falling... (asleep) quickly.
A week later, she felt energetic and no longer needed as much... (coffee).
She realized that sleep is the foundation of health and is necessary to... (recharge).

Тема: № 85 Питание и энергичность в течение дня (Nutrition and Daily Energy)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. energy levels [ˈenədʒi ˈlevlz] — уровень энергии
2. to crash [kræʃ] — резко терять силы, "выключаться"
3. balanced diet [ˈbælənst ˈdaɪət] — сбалансированная диета
4. hydration [haɪˈdreɪʃn] — гидратация, водный баланс
5. processed food [ˈprəʊsest fuːd] — обработанная пища
6. to fuel [fjuːəl] — подпитывать, давать энергию

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~430 слов.
It was 3:00 PM, and Mark was staring blankly at his computer screen. He yawned for the fifth time in an hour and reached for his third cup of coffee. 

"You look like a zombie, Mark," said Sarah, walking past his desk with a large bottle of water and a green apple. "Rough afternoon?"

"I'm crashing hard," Mark admitted, rubbing his eyes. "I had a huge pasta lunch at noon, and now I can barely keep my eyes open. How do you always have so much energy?"

Sarah pulled up a chair. "It’s not magic, it’s biology. You fueled your body with heavy, processed carbohydrates. They give you a quick spike in blood sugar, followed by a massive crash. That’s why you’re tired."

"So, what’s the secret?" Mark asked, pushing his coffee mug away. 

"A balanced diet and proper hydration," Sarah explained. "I eat a lunch with lean protein, healthy fats, and vegetables. It gives me steady energy all day. And I drink at least two liters of water. Dehydration is a major cause of afternoon fatigue."

Mark looked at his empty coffee cup. "I probably drink more coffee than water."

"That’s making it worse," Sarah smiled. "Caffeine is a temporary fix. Try this: tomorrow, swap your heavy lunch for a chicken salad, keep a water bottle on your desk, and eat a handful of nuts instead of a candy bar at 3 PM."

"Will that really make a difference?" Mark was skeptical.

"Just try it for three days," Sarah challenged him. "Your brain needs the right fuel to focus. You can't drive a car on empty, and you can't work on junk food."

The next day, Mark decided to follow Sarah’s advice. He brought a turkey wrap and a large bottle of water to the office. At 3:00 PM, instead of reaching for coffee, he ate an apple and some almonds. 

To his surprise, he didn't feel the usual heavy slump. He was able to finish his report without yawning once. 

On Friday, Mark walked into the kitchen and poured himself a glass of water. Sarah walked in and raised an eyebrow. "Look at you! No coffee?"

"Nope," Mark grinned. "You were right. My energy levels are so much more stable. I feel like I actually have control over my day now."

Sarah smiled. "See? Small changes in what you eat and drink can completely transform how you work and feel."

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why was Mark feeling so tired at 3:00 PM?
2. What did Sarah suggest Mark eat and drink to improve his energy?
3. How did Mark feel after trying Sarah’s advice for a few days?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Mark was struggling with low... (energy levels) and wanted to... (crash) after a heavy lunch.
Sarah explained that processed food causes a sugar spike and a... (crash).
She advised him to follow a... (balanced diet) and maintain proper... (hydration).
Mark decided to... (fuel) his body with a turkey wrap, water, and nuts.
After three days, he noticed his energy was much more... (stable).
He realized that small changes in nutrition can transform how you... (work / feel).

Тема: № 86 Цифровая гигиена и управление вниманием (Digital Hygiene and Attention Management)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. digital detox [ˈdɪdʒɪtl ˈdiːtɒks] — цифровой детокс
2. notification [ˌnəʊtɪfɪˈkeɪʃn] — уведомление
3. attention span [əˈtenʃn spæn] — продолжительность концентрации внимания
4. to scroll [skrəʊl] — пролистывать (ленту)
5. focus mode [ˈfəʊkəs məʊd] — режим фокусировки
6. to reclaim [rɪˈkleɪm] — возвращать, отвоевывать

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~440 слов.
"Alex, I’ve been trying to talk to you for five minutes," Elena said, standing by his desk. 

Alex jumped, quickly minimizing a browser tab. "Oh, sorry, Elena! I was just checking a quick message."

Elena sighed gently. "Alex, your phone buzzes every three minutes. You check your email, then Slack, then Instagram. Your attention span is completely fragmented. You’re not working; you’re just reacting."

Alex looked defensive. "But I have to stay connected! What if a client needs me urgently?"

"Urgent things are rare," Elena replied. "Most of those notifications are just noise. They are destroying your ability to do deep, meaningful work. Have you ever heard of a digital detox?"

"A detox? You mean not using my phone at all?" Alex asked, looking horrified.

"Not forever. Just setting boundaries," Elena explained. "For example, turn off all non-essential notifications. Put your phone in another room when you need to write a report. Use 'focus mode' on your computer to block distracting websites for two hours."

"That sounds extreme," Alex muttered. 

"Try it for just one morning," Elena challenged. "I promise, you will reclaim at least two hours of productive time. And you’ll feel much less stressed."

The next morning, Alex decided to try it. He turned off all social media notifications. He put his phone in his desk drawer. He activated focus mode on his laptop. 

For the first twenty minutes, he felt a strong urge to check his phone. His hand twitched toward the drawer. But he forced himself to focus on the spreadsheet in front of him. 

Slowly, the urge faded. He entered a state of deep concentration. He finished a complex report in 90 minutes—a task that usually took him half a day. 

At 11:00 AM, he opened his drawer and checked his phone. There were no emergencies. Just a few memes from a friend and a routine email from HR. 

He walked over to Elena’s desk, smiling. "You were right. It was hard at first, but I got so much done. I feel like I’m in control of my time again."

Elena smiled back. "That’s the power of digital hygiene. You don’t control the technology; you control how you use it."

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why was Elena frustrated with Alex at the beginning of the story?
2. What is a "digital detox" according to Elena?
3. How did Alex feel during the first twenty minutes of his experiment?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Elena noticed that Alex’s... (attention span) was fragmented due to constant... (notifications).
She suggested he try a... (digital detox) by setting boundaries.
Alex was skeptical but agreed to turn off alerts and use... (focus mode).
At first, he felt a strong urge to... (scroll) or check his phone.
However, he resisted and managed to finish a complex report in just... (90 minutes).
He checked his phone later and realized there were no... (emergencies).
Alex learned that digital hygiene helps you... (reclaim) your time and reduce stress.

Тема: № 87 Публичные выступления и страх сцены (углублённо) (Public Speaking and Stage Fright - Advanced)
Уровень: B1-B2 (Pre-Intermediate / Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. stage fright [steɪdʒ fraɪt] — страх сцены
2. rehearsal [rɪˈhɜːsl] — репетиция
3. visualization [ˌvɪʒuəlaɪˈzeɪʃn] — визуализация
4. to stumble [ˈstʌmbl] — запинаться, оговариваться
5. pacing [ˈpeɪsɪŋ] — темп речи
6. audience engagement [ˈɔːdiəns ɪnˈɡeɪdʒmənt] — вовлечение аудитории

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~450 слов.
David paced back and forth in the empty conference room, his hands shaking slightly. "I can't do this, Maria. The regional conference is tomorrow, and I’m going to freeze up. My stage fright is unbearable."

Maria, his presentation coach, sat calmly in the front row. "David, take a seat. Let’s do one more rehearsal. But this time, we are going to focus on your mindset, not just the words."

David sat down, breathing heavily. "What if I stumble? What if I forget my lines?"

"Then you pause, take a breath, and continue," Maria said firmly. "The audience doesn't have your script. They won't know you made a mistake unless you panic. Let’s try visualization. Close your eyes."

David closed his eyes. 

"Imagine walking onto the stage," Maria guided him. "You feel confident. Your pacing is steady and calm. You make eye contact with a friendly face in the front row. You are not performing; you are sharing valuable information. How does that feel?"

"Better," David whispered. "But what about audience engagement? I’m afraid they will be bored."

"Ask them a question," Maria suggested. "Start your speech with: 'How many of you have ever felt overwhelmed by data?' It immediately connects you to them. It turns a monologue into a conversation."

David opened his eyes and stood up. "Okay. Let’s try the opening again."

He took a deep breath, smiled, and began. "Good morning. How many of you have ever felt overwhelmed by data?" He paused, looking directly at Maria. His voice was strong, and his pacing was perfect. He didn't stumble once.

"Excellent!" Maria clapped. "That was powerful. You are ready, David. Remember, the audience wants you to succeed. They are on your side."

The next day, David stood on the real stage. The lights were bright, and his heart raced for a second. But then he remembered the visualization. He took a deep breath, smiled, and asked his opening question. 

The audience responded immediately, nodding and smiling. David felt a wave of confidence. He delivered the entire presentation smoothly, engaging the crowd and answering questions with ease.

Afterward, several attendees came up to thank him. "That was the most engaging talk of the day," one manager said.

David walked out of the conference hall feeling a massive sense of achievement. He realized that stage fright never completely disappears, but with preparation, visualization, and a focus on the audience, it can be completely managed.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why was David so nervous before the regional conference?
2. What technique did Maria use to help David calm down?
3. How did David start his speech to improve audience engagement?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, David was suffering from severe... (stage fright) before his big presentation.
Maria told him to do a... (rehearsal) focusing on his mindset.
She guided him through a... (visualization) exercise to imagine success.
She advised him to ask a question at the start to boost... (audience engagement).
David practiced his opening, focusing on steady... (pacing) and not being afraid to... (stumble).
On the real stage, he used the visualization, took a deep breath, and spoke confidently.
The audience loved the talk, and David learned that preparation can help... (manage) fear.

Тема: № 88 Нетворкинг и построение профессиональных связей (Networking and Building Professional Connections)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. networking [ˈnetwɜːkɪŋ] — нетворкинг, установление связей
2. elevator pitch [ˈelɪveɪtə pɪtʃ] — краткая самопрезентация (лифтовая речь)
3. to mingle [ˈmɪŋɡl] — общаться, смешиваться (на мероприятии)
4. genuine interest [ˈdʒenjuɪn ˈɪntrəst] — искренний интерес
5. to follow up [ˈfɒləʊ ʌp] — поддерживать связь, напоминать о себе
6. to expand [ɪkˈspænd] — расширять

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~440 слов.
Julia stood in the corner of the busy conference hall, holding a glass of water. She felt completely out of place. Everyone seemed to know each other, laughing and exchanging business cards. 

"Networking is a nightmare," she muttered to herself. "I don't know what to say."

"First time at a big industry event?" asked a friendly voice. 

Julia turned to see Tom, a senior manager from her company. "Yes. I feel like I’m bothering people if I just walk up to them."

Tom smiled. "That’s a common fear. But networking isn’t about selling yourself; it’s about building relationships. Do you have your elevator pitch ready?"

"My what?" Julia asked.

"Your elevator pitch," Tom explained. "A 30-second summary of who you are, what you do, and what you’re looking for. For example: 'Hi, I’m Julia, a UX designer. I specialize in making apps user-friendly, and I’m currently exploring new trends in AI design.'"

"That sounds professional," Julia said, repeating it quietly. "But how do I start a conversation?"

"Ask open-ended questions and show genuine interest," Tom advised. "Instead of talking about yourself, ask them: 'What brought you to this conference?' or 'What’s the most interesting project you’re working on?' People love talking about their work."

"Okay, I’ll try," Julia said, taking a deep breath. 

She spotted a small group near the coffee station. She walked over and smiled. "Hi, I’m Julia. I’m a UX designer. What brought you to this conference?"

A woman named Rachel turned and smiled back. "Hi Julia! I’m Rachel, a product manager. I’m here to learn more about user retention strategies. How about you?"

Julia delivered her elevator pitch smoothly. Rachel’s eyes lit up. "Actually, we’re looking for a UX designer for a new project. We should connect on LinkedIn."

They talked for ten minutes, and Julia felt completely relaxed. She wasn't selling; she was just having a normal conversation. 

The next day, Julia sent Rachel a message: "Hi Rachel, it was great meeting you yesterday. I’d love to follow up on our conversation about user retention. Let me know if you have 15 minutes for a virtual coffee next week."

Rachel replied within an hour: "I’d love that! Let’s schedule it."

Julia showed the message to Tom. "It worked! I expanded my network, and I didn’t even feel awkward."

"See?" Tom said. "Networking is just making friends with a professional purpose. You did great."

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why did Julia feel uncomfortable at the conference?
2. What is an "elevator pitch" according to Tom?
3. How did Julia successfully start a conversation with Rachel?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Julia was afraid of... (networking) and didn't know how to... (mingle).
Tom advised her to prepare a 30-second... (elevator pitch).
He also told her to show... (genuine interest) by asking open-ended questions.
Julia approached a group and asked Rachel what brought her to the... (conference).
They had a great conversation, and Rachel suggested they connect on... (LinkedIn).
The next day, Julia decided to... (follow up) with a message.
Rachel agreed to meet, and Julia successfully managed to... (expand) her professional network.

Тема: № 89 Личный бренд и самопрезентация (Personal Brand and Self-Presentation)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. personal brand [ˈpɜːsənl brænd] — личный бренд
2. consistency [kənˈsɪstənsi] — последовательность
3. online presence [ˌɒnlaɪn ˈprezns] — присутствие в интернете
4. to showcase [ˈʃəʊkeɪs] — демонстрировать, показывать
5. authenticity [ˌɔːθenˈtɪsəti] — подлинность, искренность
6. reputation [ˌrepjuˈteɪʃn] — репутация

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~430 слов.
"Leo, your quarterly results are fantastic," said Anna, the department director. "But I have to be honest. When the executive team discusses promotions, your name rarely comes up. You’re a bit invisible."

Leo looked surprised and a bit hurt. "But I work harder than anyone! I stay late, I finish all my projects on time. Why isn't that enough?"

"Hard work is essential, Leo," Anna explained gently. "But in today’s world, you also need a strong personal brand. If people don't know what you do and the value you bring, your reputation remains limited to our immediate team."

"How do I build a personal brand? I’m not an influencer," Leo said, frowning.

"It’s not about being famous," Anna smiled. "It’s about consistency and authenticity. It’s about making sure the right people see your expertise. For example, do you have an updated LinkedIn profile?"

"I have one, but I haven’t touched it in two years," Leo admitted.

"That’s your first step," Anna said. "Update it. Showcase your recent projects. Write a short post about a problem you solved last month. Share your knowledge."

"Will people actually read that?" Leo asked skeptically.

"Yes, especially if you are authentic," Anna replied. "Don’t try to sound like a corporate robot. Write like you speak. Share your genuine thoughts on industry trends. And inside the company, speak up in meetings. Share your ideas confidently."

Leo decided to take her advice. That weekend, he completely revamped his LinkedIn profile. He added a professional photo, detailed his achievements, and wrote a short article about a new software tool he had mastered. 

The following week, during a team meeting, Leo didn’t sit silently in the back. When a problem arose, he raised his hand. "I actually have some experience with this," he said clearly. "Here is a solution that worked for me on a previous project."

The director nodded, impressed. "That’s a great idea, Leo. Let’s explore that."

A month later, Leo received a message on LinkedIn from a recruiter at another company, and his own manager recommended him for a cross-departmental leadership role. 

"See?" Anna said, smiling at him. "Your online presence and your confidence in meetings are working. You are no longer invisible."

Leo realized that a personal brand wasn’t about bragging. It was about consistently and authentically showcasing your value so that opportunities could find you.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why did Anna say Leo was "invisible" despite his hard work?
2. What two qualities did Anna say are important for a personal brand?
3. What specific actions did Leo take to improve his personal brand?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Anna told Leo that hard work is not enough; he needs a strong... (personal brand).
She explained that it is about... (consistency) and... (authenticity).
Leo’s first step was to update his... (online presence) on LinkedIn.
He decided to... (showcase) his recent projects and share his knowledge.
In meetings, he started to speak up and share his... (ideas) confidently.
Soon, he got attention from a recruiter and a recommendation for a new... (role).
Leo learned that a personal brand is about... (showcasing) your value so opportunities find you.

Тема: № 90 Эмоциональный интеллект в общении (Emotional Intelligence in Communication)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. empathy [ˈempəθi] — эмпатия, сопереживание
2. active listening [ˈæktɪv ˈlɪsnɪŋ] — активное слушание
3. self-regulation [ˌself ˌreɡjuˈleɪʃn] — саморегуляция, контроль эмоций
4. to validate [ˈvælɪdeɪt] — признавать (чувства), подтверждать
5. non-verbal cues [nɒn ˈvɜːbl kjuːz] — невербальные сигналы
6. to respond [rɪˈspɒnd] — реагировать (осознанно), отвечать

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~440 слов.
The phone rang, and Sarah knew immediately it was going to be a difficult call. It was Mr. Brown, a long-term client, and his voice was loud and angry.

"Sarah, this is unacceptable! The delivery is three days late, and my entire production line is waiting. I want to speak to your manager right now!"

Sarah felt a surge of panic and defensiveness. Her first instinct was to argue: "It’s not my fault, the courier delayed it!" But she took a deep breath. She remembered her training in emotional intelligence. 

First, self-regulation. She calmed her own racing heart. She knew that reacting with anger would only make the situation worse. She needed to respond, not react.

"Mr. Brown, I hear how frustrated you are," Sarah said, keeping her voice calm and steady. "A three-day delay is incredibly stressful, especially when your production line is waiting. I completely understand why you are upset."

There was a pause on the other end. Mr. Brown had expected a fight, but Sarah had just used empathy to validate his feelings. 

"Well, yes, it is stressful," he said, his voice slightly softer. "What are you going to do about it?"

"Let me check the tracking system right now," Sarah said, practicing active listening and focusing entirely on his problem. She noticed his tone shifting from anger to anxious expectation. She also paid attention to her own non-verbal cues, making sure she was sitting up straight and speaking clearly, even though he couldn't see her.

"Okay, Mr. Brown," Sarah said a moment later. "I see the issue. The truck broke down, but I have already arranged for an express replacement shipment. It will arrive at your facility tomorrow morning at 8 AM, at no extra cost to you."

"Tomorrow morning?" Mr. Brown asked. "Are you sure?"

"Absolutely," Sarah confirmed. "I will personally monitor the tracking and call you at 7 AM to confirm it’s on the way."

"Alright," Mr. Brown sighed, sounding relieved. "Thank you, Sarah. I appreciate you handling this so quickly. I’m sorry I raised my voice."

"No need to apologize," Sarah smiled. "We value your business, and I’m glad we could resolve this."

After hanging up, Sarah’s colleague, Mark, looked at her in amazement. "How did you calm him down so fast? I would have yelled back."

"Emotional intelligence," Sarah said. "I regulated my own emotions, validated his frustration, and focused on solving the problem. It’s always more effective than arguing."

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why was Mr. Brown angry when he called Sarah?
2. What was Sarah’s first instinct, and what did she do instead?
3. How did Sarah use empathy to change the tone of the conversation?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Mr. Brown called, angry about a three-day... (delivery) delay.
Sarah felt defensive but used... (self-regulation) to calm down.
She decided to... (respond) thoughtfully instead of reacting with anger.
She used... (empathy) to... (validate) his frustration, which surprised him.
Practicing... (active listening), she checked the system and found a solution.
She arranged an express shipment and promised to monitor it... (personally).
Mr. Brown calmed down and apologized, and Sarah proved that... (emotional intelligence) is more effective than arguing.

Тема: № 91 Конфликтные ситуации в личной жизни (Conflict Situations in Personal Life)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. to argue [ˈɑːɡjuː] — спорить, ссориться
2. misunderstanding [ˌmɪsʌndəˈstændɪŋ] — недоразумение
3. to apologize [əˈpɒlədʒaɪz] — извиняться
4. to forgive [fəˈɡɪv] — прощать
5. to make up [meɪk ʌp] — мириться
6. to listen actively [ˈlɪsn ˈæktɪvli] — слушать внимательно, активно слушать

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~420 слов.
It was Friday evening, and the atmosphere in the kitchen was tense. Anna and Mark had been arguing for twenty minutes about their weekend plans.

"I thought we agreed to visit my parents this Saturday!" Anna said, her voice rising. "You promised!"

Mark sighed, running a hand through his hair. "I didn't promise anything. I said we might go if I finish my project. But I’m exhausted, Anna. I just want to stay home and rest."

"Well, I already told them we’re coming," Anna replied, crossing her arms. "Now I look like a liar because of you."

"This is a classic misunderstanding," Mark said, trying to stay calm. "You heard what you wanted to hear. I clearly said 'maybe'."

Anna felt tears welling up in her eyes. She turned away, feeling hurt and angry. The argument was going in circles, and neither of them was listening. 

Suddenly, Mark took a deep breath. He realized that winning the argument was less important than their relationship. He walked over and gently touched her shoulder.

"Anna, I’m sorry," he said softly. "I shouldn't have raised my voice. Let’s stop arguing and talk properly. I will listen actively this time."

Anna turned around, surprised by his tone. She took a deep breath too. "Okay. I’m sorry I snapped at you. I know you’ve had a hard week. I just felt disappointed because I was looking forward to seeing them."

"I understand," Mark said. "And I appreciate that. But I am completely burned out. If I go, I’ll be in a terrible mood, and that won’t be fair to your parents or to you."

Anna thought for a moment. "What if we go for just two hours on Sunday morning? You can rest all day Saturday, and we’ll bring lunch so your mom doesn’t have to cook."

Mark smiled, feeling a huge wave of relief. "That sounds like a perfect compromise. Thank you for understanding."

"Thank you for apologizing," Anna smiled back. "Let's make up and order some pizza."

"Deal," Mark said, pulling out his phone. 

They sat on the couch, eating pizza and watching a movie. The tension was completely gone. They realized that conflicts are a normal part of any relationship. The key is not to avoid them, but to handle them with respect, active listening, and a willingness to apologize and forgive.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why were Anna and Mark arguing on Friday evening?
2. How did Mark stop the argument from going in circles?
3. What compromise did they reach about the weekend plans?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Anna and Mark were arguing about their... (weekend plans).
Anna thought Mark promised to visit her parents, but it was a... (misunderstanding).
Mark realized they needed to stop arguing and started to... (listen actively).
He decided to... (apologize) for raising his voice, and Anna apologized too.
They found a compromise: resting on Saturday and visiting for two hours on... (Sunday).
They decided to... (make up) and order pizza.
They learned that handling conflicts requires respect and a willingness to... (forgive).

Тема: № 92 Построение здоровых отношений (Building Healthy Relationships)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. trust [trʌst] — доверие
2. boundaries [ˈbaʊndəriz] — личные границы
3. to support [səˈpɔːt] — поддерживать
4. quality time [ˈkwɒləti taɪm] — качественное время (вместе)
5. honest [ˈɒnɪst] — честный, искренний
6. to appreciate [əˈpriːʃieɪt] — ценить, быть благодарным

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~430 слов.
"David, we need to talk about us," Emma said, sitting down on the sofa. She looked serious but calm.

David put his phone away immediately. "Is everything okay? You sound worried."

"We are good," Emma smiled gently. "But I’ve been reading about healthy relationships, and I want to make sure we are building a strong foundation. Do you feel like we have enough trust and honest communication?"

David thought for a moment. "I think we do. We tell each other everything. But sometimes I feel like I don’t give you enough quality time. I’ve been working late a lot."

"I appreciate your hard work," Emma said, taking his hand. "But I also need to set some boundaries. When you work at the dinner table, I feel ignored. Can we agree that from 7 PM to 9 PM, we have no screens? Just us, talking or watching a movie."

"That is a fantastic idea," David agreed. "I can definitely support that. It will help me disconnect from work, too. What else?"

"I think we need to appreciate each other more," Emma continued. "We get so busy that we forget to say 'thank you' for small things, like making coffee or doing the laundry."

"You’re right," David admitted. "I take you for granted sometimes. How about we start a new habit? Every evening at dinner, we share one thing we appreciated about each other that day."

Emma’s face lit up. "I love that! It’s so simple, but it will make a huge difference."

They tried the new rules for a month. The "no screens" rule was hard at first, but soon they found themselves talking about their dreams, fears, and funny stories from work. The daily appreciation habit made them feel seen and valued.

One evening, David said, "You know, Emma, I feel closer to you now than I have in years. Setting boundaries didn’t push us apart; it actually brought us closer."

Emma smiled. "That’s because healthy relationships aren’t about being together 24/7. They are about respecting each other’s space, being honest about your needs, and actively choosing to support each other every single day."

David nodded, feeling grateful. They realized that love is not just a feeling; it’s a series of daily actions that build trust and connection.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. What boundary did Emma want to set regarding their evenings?
2. What new daily habit did David suggest to show appreciation?
3. How did David feel about their relationship after a month of trying the new rules?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Emma wanted to talk about building a healthy relationship based on... (trust).
She suggested setting... (boundaries), like having no screens from 7 to 9 PM.
David agreed and said he would... (support) this idea.
They also decided to... (appreciate) each other by sharing one good thing every evening.
This habit helped them spend more... (quality time) together.
After a month, David felt much... (closer) to Emma.
They learned that healthy relationships require respect, honesty, and daily... (actions).

Тема: № 93 Самомотивация и преодоление прокрастинации (Self-Motivation and Overcoming Procrastination)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. motivation [ˌməʊtɪˈveɪʃn] — мотивация
2. to overcome [ˌəʊvəˈkʌm] — преодолевать
3. goal [ɡəʊl] — цель
4. reward [rɪˈwɔːd] — награда, вознаграждение
5. to distract [dɪˈstrækt] — отвлекать
6. to break down [breɪk daʊn] — разбивать на части

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~440 слов.
Tom stared at the blank document on his screen. He had to write a 20-page report by Friday, but he had spent the last two hours watching funny videos on his phone. 

"I have zero motivation," he groaned, putting his head on the desk. 

His roommate, Alex, walked into the room holding two cups of coffee. "Still procrastinating on that report?"

"Yes," Tom admitted. "It’s too big. I don’t even know where to start, so I just distract myself with social media."

Alex handed him a coffee. "That’s normal. When a goal is too big, your brain panics and avoids it. You need to break it down into tiny, manageable steps."

"Like what?" Tom asked, sitting up.

"Instead of 'write a 20-page report', your first goal is 'write the title and three bullet points for the introduction'. That takes five minutes. Can you do that?"

Tom sighed. "Yeah, I guess I can do that."

"Great. Do it now. And when you finish those five minutes, give yourself a small reward. Maybe a piece of chocolate or a 10-minute walk."

Tom opened the document. He wrote the title and three bullet points. It took him exactly four minutes. He felt a tiny spark of achievement. He ate a piece of chocolate and felt slightly better.

"See?" Alex said. "Now, your next step is to write one paragraph for the first bullet point. Just one. Set a timer for 25 minutes and don’t look at your phone."

Tom set the timer. He focused only on that one paragraph. Without the pressure of the whole 20 pages, he found it easy to start. When the timer rang, he had written half a page. 

Over the next two days, Tom used this method. He broke the report into small sections, worked in 25-minute blocks, and rewarded himself after each one. He kept his phone in another room to avoid distractions.

By Thursday afternoon, the report was finished. Tom leaned back in his chair, feeling incredibly proud. 

"Alex, it worked!" Tom called out. "I actually overcame my procrastination."

Alex smiled from the kitchen. "I told you. Motivation doesn’t come before action. Action creates motivation. You just had to trick your brain into starting."

Tom realized that overcoming procrastination wasn’t about having superhuman willpower. It was about breaking big tasks into small steps, eliminating distractions, and celebrating small wins along the way.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why was Tom procrastinating on his report?
2. What advice did Alex give Tom to start working?
3. How did Tom reward himself for completing small steps?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Tom had no... (motivation) to write a 20-page report.
He kept trying to... (distract) himself with his phone.
Alex advised him to... (break down) the big task into tiny steps.
Tom’s first... (goal) was to write just the title and three bullet points.
After finishing a small step, he gave himself a... (reward), like chocolate.
He used a timer to focus and avoid... (distractions).
By Thursday, Tom finished the report and learned how to... (overcome) procrastination.
He realized that action creates... (motivation).

Тема: № 94 Минимализм и осознанное потребление (Minimalism and Conscious Consumption)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. minimalism [ˈmɪnɪməlɪzəm] — минимализм
2. to declutter [diːˈklʌtə] — избавляться от хлама, наводить порядок
3. unnecessary [ʌnˈnesəsəri] — ненужный
4. to donate [dəʊˈneɪt] — жертвовать, отдавать на благотворительность
5. mindful [ˈmaɪndfʊl] — осознанный, внимательный
6. to value [ˈvæljuː] — ценить

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~430 слов.
Sarah stood in the middle of her bedroom, surrounded by piles of clothes, old books, and gadgets she hadn’t used in years. She felt overwhelmed. 

"I can’t find anything in this mess," she muttered to herself. 

Her friend, Lisa, came over to help. "Wow, Sarah. You have a lot of stuff. Have you ever thought about trying minimalism?"

"Minimalism?" Sarah asked, holding up a sweater with the tags still on. "You mean living with almost nothing? I could never do that."

"It’s not about having nothing," Lisa explained. "It’s about keeping only the things that bring you joy or are truly useful. It’s about being mindful of what you buy and own. Let’s declutter this room together."

Sarah hesitated, but then nodded. "Okay. Where do we start?"

"Let’s make three boxes," Lisa said. "Keep, Donate, and Throw away. Be honest. When was the last time you wore this sweater?"

"Never," Sarah admitted. "I bought it on sale, but it doesn’t fit my style."

"Then it’s unnecessary," Lisa said. "Put it in the Donate box. Someone else will value it."

For the next three hours, they sorted through everything. Sarah was surprised by how many unnecessary items she had accumulated. Old magazines, broken chargers, clothes that didn’t fit. 

By the end of the day, they had filled four large bags for donation and two bags for recycling. The room looked completely different. It was spacious, clean, and calm.

Sarah sat on her bed and took a deep breath. "I feel so much lighter. I didn’t realize how much my stuff was stressing me out."

"That’s the magic of decluttering," Lisa smiled. "When you remove the unnecessary, you make space for what truly matters. And next time you want to buy something, ask yourself: 'Do I really need this, or will it just become more clutter?'"

"I will definitely think twice before shopping now," Sarah said. "I want to be a mindful consumer."

A week later, Sarah went to the charity shop to drop off the bags. She felt a warm sense of satisfaction knowing her old things would help others. 

She returned to her clean, peaceful apartment and realized that minimalism wasn’t about deprivation. It was about freedom. By choosing to value experiences and relationships over material things, she had created a life that felt truly rich.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. Why did Sarah feel overwhelmed in her bedroom?
2. What three boxes did Lisa suggest using for decluttering?
3. How did Sarah feel after cleaning her room?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Sarah was surrounded by mess and couldn't find anything.
Lisa suggested she try... (minimalism) to feel better.
They decided to... (declutter) the room using three boxes: Keep, Donate, and Throw away.
Sarah realized she had many... (unnecessary) items, like clothes with tags.
They filled several bags to... (donate) to charity.
After cleaning, Sarah felt much... (lighter) and less stressed.
She decided to become a... (mindful) consumer and think twice before buying new things.
She learned that minimalism is about freedom and choosing to... (value) experiences over things.

Тема: № 95 Планирование года и постановка долгосрочных целей (Yearly Planning and Setting Long-Term Goals)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. long-term goal [lɒŋ tɜːm ɡəʊl] — долгосрочная цель
2. to achieve [əˈtʃiːv] — достигать
3. vision board [ˈvɪʒn bɔːd] — доска визуализации
4. measurable [ˈmeʒərəbl] — измеримый
5. to track [træk] — отслеживать
6. milestone [ˈmaɪlstəʊn] — важный этап, веха

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~440 слов.
It was December 30th, and John and his wife, Maria, were sitting at the kitchen table with cups of hot tea. 

"Next year is going to be different," John said, opening a fresh notebook. "Last year, we just let time pass. This year, I want to set some real goals."

Maria smiled. "I love that idea. But how do we make sure we actually achieve them? We always make resolutions and forget them by February."

"We need a system," John replied. "First, let’s define our main long-term goal. Where do we want to be in three years?"

Maria thought for a moment. "I think we want to buy a small house in the countryside and have enough savings for a big trip."

"Perfect," John said, writing it down. "Now, we need to make this goal measurable. Instead of 'save money', let’s say 'save $10,000 by December next year'. That’s about $850 a month."

"That sounds doable," Maria agreed. "But how do we remember to do it?"

"We create a vision board," John suggested. "We can print pictures of the house we want, the places we want to visit, and put them on the fridge. Every time we see it, we remember why we are saving."

"Great idea," Maria said. "And we should track our progress. Maybe we can have a monthly 'money date' to review our budget and celebrate when we hit a milestone."

"Exactly," John smiled. "And it’s not just about money. What is one personal goal for you?"

"I want to run a 10K race," Maria said. "So my measurable goal is to run three times a week and finish the race in October."

"Awesome. I will support you," John said. "And my goal is to read 24 books this year. That’s two books a month."

They spent the next hour cutting out pictures, writing down their measurable goals, and sticking them onto a large poster board. It looked colorful and inspiring.

"Look at this," Maria said, stepping back to admire their work. "It makes the future feel so real and exciting."

"It does," John agreed. "We aren’t just wishing for things anymore. We have a plan, we know how to track our progress, and we will celebrate every milestone together."

They clinked their tea mugs. They realized that successful planning wasn’t about making a strict, boring list. It was about creating a shared vision, breaking it down into measurable steps, and supporting each other every day.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. What was John and Maria’s main long-term goal for the next three years?
2. How did they make their financial goal measurable?
3. What personal goals did Maria and John set for themselves?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, John and Maria decided to set real goals for the new year.
They defined their main... (long-term goal): buying a house and saving for a trip.
They made it... (measurable) by deciding to save $850 a month.
To stay motivated, they created a... (vision board) with pictures.
They agreed to... (track) their progress with a monthly "money date".
They also set personal goals, like running a 10K race and reading... (24 books).
They realized that planning is about creating a shared vision and celebrating every... (milestone).

Тема: № 96 Рефлексия и подведение итогов (Reflection and Reviewing Results)
Уровень: B1 (Pre-Intermediate)

1. Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 6 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1. reflection [rɪˈflekʃn] — рефлексия, размышление
2. to review [rɪˈvjuː] — пересматривать, подводить итоги
3. lesson learned [ˈlesn lɜːnd] — извлеченный урок
4. to improve [ɪmˈpruːv] — улучшать
5. success [səkˈses] — успех
6. to evaluate [ɪˈvæljueɪt] — оценивать

2. The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами. Объем: ~430 слов.
It was the last Sunday of the year. Emma sat at her desk with a cup of coffee and a thick journal. It was time for her annual reflection.

"Okay, 2023, let’s see what we’ve got," she said to herself, opening the notebook. 

She decided to review the year in three parts: successes, failures, and lessons learned. 

First, she wrote down her successes. "I got promoted at work. I ran my first half-marathon. I saved enough money for a new laptop." She smiled as she wrote. It felt good to acknowledge her achievements.

Next came the hard part: evaluating the failures. "I didn’t learn Spanish like I planned. I argued with my sister in the summer. I spent too much time on social media."

She felt a pang of guilt, but she reminded herself that reflection is not about self-criticism. It’s about understanding what happened. 

"Why didn’t I learn Spanish?" she asked herself. "Because I tried to study for an hour every day, and I always got too tired after work. That was unrealistic."

She wrote down the lesson learned: "Next year, I will study for just 15 minutes a day. Small, consistent steps are better than big, impossible plans."

She did the same for the other failures. The argument with her sister taught her to communicate better. The social media habit taught her to set strict time limits.

Her brother, Tom, walked into the room. "Doing your yearly review?" he asked, sitting on the edge of the bed.

"Yes," Emma said. "It’s actually really helpful. I used to just feel bad about what I didn’t do. But now I see how much I’ve grown, and I know exactly what to improve next year."

"That’s the point of reflection," Tom said. "You can’t improve if you don’t evaluate where you are. What’s your main focus for next year?"

"Health and learning," Emma replied. "But this time, my goals will be realistic and kind to myself."

Tom smiled. "That sounds like a perfect plan."

Emma closed her journal, feeling a deep sense of peace. She realized that reviewing the past wasn’t about living in it. It was about gathering wisdom, celebrating success, and using every lesson learned to build a better, more intentional future.

3. Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1. What three parts did Emma use to review her year?
2. Why did Emma fail to learn Spanish, according to her reflection?
3. What lesson did Emma learn from her social media habit?

Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану (Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи):
First, Emma sat down with her journal for her annual... (reflection).
She decided to... (review) the year by looking at successes, failures, and lessons learned.
She wrote down her... (success), like getting a promotion.
When evaluating failures, she realized her Spanish goal was... (unrealistic).
The... (lesson learned) was to study for just 15 minutes a day.
Her brother Tom agreed that you must... (evaluate) your current state to... (improve).
Emma realized that reflection is about gathering wisdom and using every... (lesson learned) to build a better future.

Тема: № 97 Аренда квартиры и общение с арендодателем ⁦(Renting an apartment and dealing with landlords)⁩
Уровень: A2-B1 ⁦(Elementary / Pre-Intermediate)⁩
1.‎Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 8 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1.‎landlord [ˈlændlɔːd] — арендодатель, хозяин квартиры
2.‎tenant [ˈtenənt] — арендатор, жилец
3.‎deposit [dɪˈpɒzɪt] — залог, депозит
4.‎lease [liːs] — договор аренды
5.‎utilities [juːˈtɪlətiz] — коммунальные услуги
6.‎to leak [liːk] — протекать
7.‎to fix [fɪks] — починить
8.‎furnished [ˈfɜːnɪʃt] — меблированный

2.‎The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами и бытовыми деталями. Объем: ~280 слов.
Anna stood in the middle of her new flat, holding her phone and a wet towel. Water was dripping from the kitchen tap onto the floor.
‎"Okay, calm down," she whispered. "It's just a tap."
‎She called her landlord, Mr. Peterson.
‎"Hello? Anna? Is everything alright?"
‎"Hi, Mr. Peterson. Sorry to bother you on Saturday. The kitchen tap is leaking. There's water everywhere."
‎"Oh no," he sighed. "Is it a slow drip or a real flood?"
‎"A slow drip, but it's been going all night. I put a bowl under it."
‎"Good girl. That's exactly what I would do. I'll send a plumber on Monday morning. Is nine o'clock okay?"
‎"Nine is perfect. Thank you." She paused. "Actually, while I have you on the phone… can I ask about the utilities? My first bill seems very high."
‎"Let me check. Does your lease include heating?"
‎"I think so. I signed a twelve-month lease, and you said the rent covers water and heating."
‎"That's right. Electricity and internet are yours, but water and heating are mine. Send me a photo of the bill, and I'll sort it out."
‎Anna felt a wave of relief. "You're a lifesaver. I was worried you'd say I have to pay it."
‎"Anna, you're a good tenant. You pay on time and you don't throw parties at 2 AM. I'm not going to argue about a bill."
‎She laughed. "Fair enough. One last thing — the sofa. Is it staying? The flat was advertised as furnished."
‎"Yes, the sofa stays. If you want it removed, just tell me, and I'll store it in the basement."
‎"Perfect. I'll keep it. My cat will love it."
‎"Alright. Plumber, Monday, nine. Have a good weekend, Anna."
‎After the call, Anna put the kettle on and sat on the old sofa. The flat was small, the tap was broken, but for the first time it felt like home.

3.‎Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1.‎What problem did Anna have in her new flat?
2.‎What did the lease say about utilities?
3.‎Why did Mr. Peterson call Anna a good tenant?

‎Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану ⁦(Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи)⁩:
First, Anna called her... ⁦(landlord)⁩ because the kitchen tap was... ⁦(leaking)⁩.
She put a... ⁦(bowl)⁩ under the tap to stop the water.
Mr. Peterson promised to send a plumber on... ⁦(Monday morning)⁩.
Anna also asked about the... ⁦(utilities)⁩ because her bill was too high.
According to the... ⁦(lease)⁩, the rent covered water and... ⁦(heating)⁩.
The landlord asked her to send a... ⁦(photo)⁩ of the bill.
Anna also asked if the flat was... ⁦(furnished)⁩ and if the sofa would stay.
In the end, the flat finally felt like... ⁦(home)⁩.

Тема: № 98 Переезд в новый город и процесс обустройства ⁦(Moving to a new city and settling in)⁩
Уровень: A2-B1 ⁦(Elementary / Pre-Intermediate)⁩
1.‎Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 8 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1.‎to move [muːv] — переезжать
2.‎cardboard box [ˈkɑːdbɔːd bɒks] — картонная коробка
3.‎to unpack [ˌʌnˈpæk] — распаковывать
4.‎neighbourhood [ˈneɪbəhʊd] — район, окрестности
5.‎to get lost [ɡet lɒst] — заблудиться
6.‎homesick [ˈhəʊmsɪk] — тоскующий по дому
7.‎to settle in [ˈsetl ɪn] — обустроиться, освоиться
8.‎to explore [ɪkˈsplɔː] — исследовать, изучать

2.‎The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами и бытовыми деталями. Объем: ~280 слов.
Tom sat on the floor surrounded by twelve cardboard boxes. He had lived in Manchester for three days, and he still couldn't find his toothbrush.
‎His phone rang. It was his sister, Kate.
‎"How's the new city?" she asked cheerfully.
‎"Honestly? I'm eating cereal with a fork because I can't find a spoon," Tom said. "And I got lost buying milk. Twice."
‎Kate laughed. "Oh, Tom. You'll settle in. Have you talked to anyone yet?"
‎"Talked to anyone? I said 'thanks' to a bus driver. That's my whole social life."
‎"Go outside," she said. "Explore the neighbourhood. Say hello to someone. You can't unpack your life from inside a box."
‎Tom sighed, put on his jacket and walked downstairs. On the corner there was a small bakery. The smell of fresh bread pulled him inside.
‎"Morning," said the woman behind the counter. "You're new, aren't you?"
‎"Is it that obvious?"
‎"You're holding a map of the city. Nobody uses paper maps anymore."
‎Tom laughed for the first time that week. "I moved here on Monday. I don't even know where the supermarket is."
‎"Two streets left, then right. And take the number 8 bus, not the number 18 — everyone makes that mistake."
‎"Thank you. I'm Tom, by the way."
‎"Maria. Welcome to the neighbourhood."
‎He bought two croissants and a loaf of bread. On the way home, an old man from his building held the door for him.
‎"You're in flat 4, right? If you need a screwdriver for that furniture, I've got three."
‎That evening, Tom finally unpacked his kitchen box. He found a spoon, made proper tea, and sat by the window.
‎He called Kate back. "I met a baker and a man with three screwdrivers."
‎"See?" she said. "You're not homesick anymore?"
‎"A little," he admitted. "But I think I'm going to like it here."

3.‎Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1.‎Why was Tom eating cereal with a fork?
2.‎What advice did his sister Kate give him?
3.‎Who did Tom meet on his first walk around the neighbourhood?

‎Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану ⁦(Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи)⁩:
First, Tom moved to a new city and sat among twelve... ⁦(cardboard boxes)⁩.
He couldn't find a spoon and he... ⁦(got lost)⁩ buying milk.
His sister told him to go outside and... ⁦(explore)⁩ the neighbourhood.
In the bakery he met a woman called... ⁦(Maria)⁩.
She told him where the... ⁦(supermarket)⁩ was and which bus to take.
An old man from his building offered him a... ⁦(screwdriver)⁩ for his furniture.
That evening Tom finally... ⁦(unpacked)⁩ his kitchen box.
He was still a bit... ⁦(homesick)⁩, but he started to like the city.

Тема: № 99 Поиск соседа по квартире и установление правил ⁦(Finding a roommate and setting house rules)⁩
Уровень: A2-B1 ⁦(Elementary / Pre-Intermediate)⁩
1.‎Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 8 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1.‎roommate [ˈruːmmeɪt] — сосед по квартире
2.‎to share [ʃeə] — делить, пользоваться вместе
3.‎house rules [haʊs ruːlz] — правила проживания
4.‎to split the bills [splɪt ðə bɪlz] — делить счета пополам
5.‎tidy [ˈtaɪdi] — аккуратный, опрятный
6.‎messy [ˈmesi] — небрежный, неаккуратный
7.‎to do the dishes [duː ðə ˈdɪʃɪz] — мыть посуду
8.‎overnight guest [ˌəʊvənaɪt ɡest] — гость с ночёвкой

2.‎The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами и бытовыми деталями. Объем: ~270 слов.
"So," said Lisa, pouring tea, "you'd be sharing the flat with me. Two bedrooms, one very small kitchen."
‎Dan looked around. It was clean, sunny, and there was a plant on every window.
‎"It's lovely. Can I ask you something honest?"
‎"Please."
‎"My last roommate was… difficult. He never did the dishes. For three weeks. I counted."
‎Lisa laughed. "Then let's talk about house rules now, not later. It saves friendships."
‎"Agreed. So — bills?"
‎"We split the bills fifty-fifty. Rent, electricity, internet. I usually pay on the first Friday of the month."
‎"Fine by me. Cleaning?"
‎"I'm quite tidy," Lisa admitted. "But I'm not your mother. How about we clean the flat together on Sunday morning? One hour, music on, done."
‎"That actually sounds nice."
‎"And the dishes?" Dan asked carefully.
‎"Whoever cooks doesn't wash up. That's the golden rule."
‎"Deal. What about guests?"
‎"Friends are welcome. But if someone stays overnight more than twice a week, tell me first. I like knowing who's in my kitchen at 7 AM."
‎"Fair. And noise? I play guitar."
‎"I love guitar. Just not at midnight on a Tuesday."
‎Dan smiled. "You know, I came here to see a room, and we're writing a constitution."
‎"Better a constitution than a war," Lisa said. "Most people say yes to the flat and never talk about the awkward things. Then they hate each other in six months."
‎"True. My last roommate and I stopped speaking over a pizza box."
‎Lisa extended her hand. "Welcome home, Dan."
‎He shook it. "When do I move in?"
‎"Saturday. Bring your guitar. And your own washing-up sponge."

3.‎Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)
Шаг 1. Ответь на простые вопросы (устно или письменно):
1.‎Why was Dan worried about sharing a flat?
2.‎What was the "golden rule" about the dishes?
3.‎What did Lisa say about overnight guests?

‎Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану ⁦(Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи)⁩:

His old... ⁦(roommate)⁩ never did the dishes for three weeks.
Lisa suggested they discuss the... ⁦(house rules)⁩ immediately.
They agreed to... ⁦(split the bills)⁩ fifty-fifty.
They decided to clean the flat together on... ⁦(Sunday morning)⁩.
The golden rule was: whoever cooks doesn't... ⁦(do the dishes)⁩.
Lisa asked Dan to tell her about any... ⁦(overnight guest)⁩.
Dan also asked about noise because he plays the... ⁦(guitar)⁩.
They shook hands and Dan agreed to... ⁦(move in)⁩ on Saturday.

Тема: № 100 Бытовой ремонт своими руками (DIY) и вызов мастера ⁦(DIY home repairs and calling a professional)⁩
Уровень: A2-B1 ⁦(Elementary / Pre-Intermediate)⁩
1.‎Vocabulary (Словарь перед чтением)
Здесь всего 8 выражений. Они помогут понять текст, но не перегрузят память.
1.‎to repair [rɪˈpeə] — ремонтировать
2.‎screwdriver [ˈskruːdraɪvə] — отвёртка
3.‎pipe [paɪp] — труба
4.‎to turn off the water [tɜːn ɒf ðə ˈwɔːtə] — перекрыть воду
5.‎toolbox [ˈtuːlbɒks] — ящик с инструментами
6.‎to make it worse [meɪk ɪt wɜːs] — сделать хуже
7.‎plumber [ˈplʌmə] — сантехник
8.‎to flood [flʌd] — затопить

2.‎The Story (История)
Текст с живыми диалогами и бытовыми деталями. Объем: ~280 слов.
"It's just a dripping pipe," Mike said confidently. "How hard can it be?"
‎His wife, Emma, looked at the toolbox in his hands. "Mike. Last month you tried to fix the toaster and we lost electricity for two hours."
‎"That was different. I've watched a video."
‎"A video."
‎"A very good video. Four minutes."
‎Emma sighed and went to work. Mike turned on his phone, watched thirty seconds, then put it away. "Right. Easy."
‎Twenty minutes later, Emma's phone rang.
‎"Em. Don't be angry."
‎"What happened?"
‎"I made it worse."
‎"How much worse?"
‎"The kitchen is… wet."
‎Emma closed her eyes. "Did you turn off the water?"
‎Silence.
‎"Mike. Did you turn off the water?"
‎"…There's a valve?"
‎"There's a valve under the sink! Turn it clockwise. Now!"
‎She heard running footsteps, a bang, and then a relieved breath. "Okay. Okay, it's stopped."
‎"Call a plumber. A real one."
‎An hour later, a calm man named Steve stood in the kitchen, looking at the pipe.
‎"Did you try to fix this yourself?" he asked politely.
‎"I watched a video," Mike said quietly.
‎Steve smiled. "Everyone watches the video." He tightened something with his hand. "Your seal was old. Ten-minute job. But you flooded half the floor, so let's dry it properly."
‎"What's the damage?" Emma asked.
‎"Sixty for the repair, plus thirty call-out fee. And a piece of advice: next time, turn off the water first."
‎After Steve left, Emma handed Mike a towel.
‎"So," she said. "Any more videos you'd like to watch?"
‎"I think I'll retire from DIY," Mike said, wiping the floor. "My talent is calling professionals."

3.‎Retelling Practice (Практика пересказа)

2.‎What important thing did Mike forget to do?
3.‎How much did the plumber charge in total?

‎Шаг 2. Пересказ по плану ⁦(Fill in the blanks / Соедини идеи)⁩:

Emma reminded him about the... ⁦(toaster)⁩ he broke last month.
Mike watched a short video and opened his... ⁦(toolbox)⁩.
Twenty minutes later he called Emma and said he... ⁦(made it worse)⁩.
He forgot to... ⁦(turn off the water)⁩ under the sink.
Emma told him to call a real... ⁦(plumber)⁩.

Mike decided to retire from... ⁦(DIY)⁩.
